NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
Tuesday, April 12, 2016 
 
PRESENT: Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor; Robert Black, Supervisor; Steve Williams, Supervisor; Jesse 
Thompson, Vice Chairman; and Jason E. Whiting, Chairman, were present.  
9:02 a.m.  Call to Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation led by Supervisor Black.  
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: There were no comments from the public.  
CONSENT AGENDA:  a) Minutes: March 22, 2016; March 22, 2016 Executive Session; and March 31 Special 
Meeting b) Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 March 2016  c) Constable Reports: Winslow Precinct #2, 
March; Snowflake Precinct #3, February & March; and Show Low Precinct #5, March 2016  d) Justice Court 
Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1, March; Winslow Precinct #2, March; Snowflake Precinct #3, March; Kayenta 
Precinct #4, March; Show Low Precinct #5, March; and Pinetop -Lakeside Precinct #6, March 2016  e) Clerk of 
the Superior Court Report March 2016  f) Juvenile Probation Report March 2016  g) Approval of 11  Back Tax 
Land Parcels sold over the counter  in the amount of $299 7.45 h) Special Event Liquor License for the White 
Mountain Hospice Foundation Inc., charitable event to be held on August 25, 2016 at the Gathering Place in 
Pinetop i) Resolution Number 04-16, cancelling the May 17, 2016 Election for the Timberland Acres Domestic 
Water Improvement District j) Resolution Number 05-16, cancelling the election on May 17, 2016 for the White 
Mountain Lakes Special Road District  k) Reissuance of Stale Dated Check  l) Application for Treasurer’s Deed 
for the State of Arizona m) Contract signed by County Manager per Board of Supervisors Resolution: Tetra Tech 
Extension of Scope of Services/Change Order for the Navajo County Pinetop Country Club Drainage Survey  – 
Tall Pines Drive and District Housing Lease Agreement for Red Dog Housing at Pinon School n) Congratulatory 
letter to Steve Best, Apache -Sitgreaves National Forest Supervisor  o) Thank you letter to Gila River Indian 
Community p) Sympathy letter to Isabel Walker and Mary Bradley for the loss of their loved ones :  Supervisor 
Whitesinger made a motion to authorize the Chairman to sign the items in the consent agenda; motion seconded 
by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
a. Presentation to Ahli Foster for winning the Division IV State Wrestling Championship in his weight class: 
Supervisor Thompson  recognized Ahli Foster for winning the Division IV State Wrestling 
Championship in his weight class, along with his coach, Coach Larson.  Supervisor Thompson advised 
Mr. Foster that he has all of the potential inside of him. Coach Larson spoke about Mr. Foster being a hard 
worker and able to see his own potential. Ahli Foster addressed the Board, indicating that his favorite take 
down is the ankle pick and that he has learned from wrestling that he can achieve anything if he puts his 
mind to it. 
b. Presentation to Ariana Haven for her State Championship in the Elk’s Hoop Shoot Competition for 12-13 
year old girls: Supervisor Whitesinger recognized Ariana Haven for her State Championship in the Elk’s 
Hoop Shoot Competition for 12 -13 year old girls and Greg Boyce , who oversees the Elk’s Hoop Shoot . 
Supervisor Whitesinger spoke about Ms. Haven’s dedication and her representation of the community 
of Cibecue. Mr. Boyce spoke about the Elk’ s Hoop Shoot Competition, which is a national competition  
that is put on by the Elk’s Lodge, for children ages 8 to 18 . He spoke about Ariana’s leadership in her 
District. He spoke about the Elk’s Hoop Shoot being the biggest intramural competition in the United 
States with over 3.3 million boys and girls participating, and that Ariana is in the top 80 in the United 
States in her age group. Supervisor Thompson congratulated Ariana.

<!-- page 2 -->
c. Presentation to Mylee Demuth for her State and Regional Championship in the Elk’s Hoop S hoot 
Competition for 10-11 year old girls and Kenadie Stuart for her State Championship in the Elk’s Hoop 
Shoot Competition for 8-9 year old girls: Chairman Whiting recognized Mylee Demuth for her State and 
Regional Championship in the Elk’s Hoop Shoot Com petition and Kenadie Stuart for her State 
Championship in the Elk’s Hoop Shoot Competition. Coach Boyce spoke about Mylee’s dedication and 
that she won first place for the region and leaves for Chicago , Illinois, on Thursday to compete in the 
National Competition. He further spoke about Kenadie Stuart and her leadership representing the State of 
Arizona.  Chairman Whiting recognized the Elk’s Lodge and all they do. 
d. Presentation to Kay Supply for winning the 2016 Small Business Development Center Success Award for 
Navajo County: Chairman Whiting recognized Tyrell Kay and Beverly Kay for winning the 2016 Small 
Business Development Center Success Award for Navajo County. Chairman Whiting spoke about how 
Kay Supply gives back to the community.  Tyrell Kay spoke about how the Small Business Development 
Center gives business es the tools necessary to succeed, including the growth tool, to help companies 
consistently grow their business and negotiate with banks. He spoke about how Small Business 
Development Center helped his business understand business a lot better and provide d resources to help 
expand the business.   
e. Consideration and possible approval of a Proclamation declaring April 10 – April 16, 2016 as “National 
Telecommunicator’s Week ”: Supervisor Williams  read the proposed proclamation and recognized 
Sheriff Clark and the dispatchers. Supervisor Williams made a motion  to approve the Proclamation 
declaring April 10 through April 16, 2016 as National Telecommunicator’s Week ; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. Sheriff Clark expressed appreciation to 
the Board for recognizing the dispatchers throughout the County.  He spoke about a dinner put on by 
Summit for the partners involved in regional communication, which will improve services and lives of the 
citizens of Navajo County . He spoke about the dispatchers and the hard work that they do. He thanked 
Donna McMonigal, stating that she is at the dispatch center 24 hours a day/7 days a week. Ms. McMonigal 
spoke of the dedication of her team to public and officer safety.  
f. Consideration and possible approval of a Proclamation declaring April 2016 “Child Abus e Prevention & 
Awareness Month”: Chairman Whiting read the proposed proclamation and recognized C.J. Wischmann 
from the Holbrook Town Council for his efforts.  Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to approve 
the proclamation declaring April 2016 Child Abuse Prevention & Awareness Month; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion. Mr. Wischmann spoke about Buena Vista and 
the work that they do. He thanked Navajo County for their support and recognition. Chairman Whiting 
recognized the work the Sheriff’s Office and County Attorney’s Office does to protect children. 
ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES : Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions : Eric Scott  
reviewed the 27-item personnel action report. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve the Personnel 
Action Report as presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session: CONSENT 
AGENDA: Supervisor Williams made a motion to enter into the Navajo County Public Health Services District 
Board of Directors Session ; motion seconded  by Supervisor Whitesinger . Vote unanimous approving the 
motion. Amendment #2 of Contract #ADHS14-071223 with the Arizona Department of Health Services for the 
Sexual Transmitted Disease (STD) Program for January 1, 2014 to December 31, 2019: Mary Herring discussed 
the item. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve Amendment #2 of Contract #ADHS14-071223 with 
the Arizona Department of Health Services for the Sexual Transmitted Disease (STD) Program for January 1, 
2014 to December 31, 2019 ; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion .

<!-- page 3 -->
Supervisor Williams made a motion to return to regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
WHITE MOUNTAIN LAKES COUNTY RECREATION IMPROVEMENT  DISTRICT: Board of 
Directors Session:  Consideration and possible appointment of White Mountain Lakes County Recreation 
Improvement District Advisory Committee Member, Lyn Misener (Jerry McGraw, Chairman of Advisory 
Committee) Chairman Whiting indicated that this item has been moved to April 26, 2016. 
COUNTY ATTORNEY: Consideration and possible approval of a Proclamation declaring April 10 – April 16, 
2016 as “Navajo County’s Crime Victims’ Rights Week: Supervisor Black read the proposed proclamation and 
recognized Roxanne Padilla and her team. Supervisor Williams made a motion  to approve the proclamation 
declaring April 10 through April 16, 2016 as Navajo County’s Crime Victims’ Rights Week ; motion seconded 
by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion. Supervisor Thompson commended the amount 
of work Navajo County has done towards making the citizens aware. Roxanne Padilla, Victims’ Rights Director, 
spoke about the stellar work that her department does and invited everyone to attend the Victim’s Rights 
Symposium on Friday , April 15, at the Snowflake NPC Campus , beginning at 8:00 a.m.  Chairman Whiting 
encouraged all to attend  the symposium. Brad Carlyon spoke about this being the 6 th annual Victim’s Rights 
Symposium and about the national speakers who will be in attendance.  Ms. Padilla spoke about the posters and 
videos which the youth in the area will be presenting during lunch, with the award winning posters and video to 
be aired on Show Low TV.  Mr. Carlyon indicated that the Symposium is sponsored by businesses and individuals 
in the community. 
TREASURER: Presentation on Investment Portfolio : Treasurer Manny Hernandez introduced Bob Moore, 
President of Institutional Capital Management.  Mr. Moore spoke about the process of developing an investment 
strategy.  He indicated that he and Treasurer Hernandez have moved away from the speculative process and have 
used the analysis of cash flow, revenue streams, expenditure patterns and the cash flows resulting therefrom and 
that a good bit of investment dollars have been moved from low yielding investments into high yielding  
investments.  Mr. Moore discussed the five-step process to reach the goals and objectives of the County.  Treasurer 
Hernandez provided a handout to members of the Board showing government debt from different countries from 
February 16, 2016.  Mr. Moore discussed the graph. Mr. Moore presented a PowerPoint presentation.  Supervisor 
Williams asked about how other counties Institutional Capital Management works. Mr. Moore indicated that he 
works with Yavapai and Gila counties, some school district s and a regional medical center in Graham County . 
Mr. Moore commended Treasurer Hernandez for the work he does and advised that Navajo County has never lost 
a dime in investments.  Supervisor Thompson stated his appreciation for Treasurer Hernandez.   
ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of a Proclamation declaring April 2016 “Fair Housing” Month for 
Navajo County : Olivia Todd read the proposed Proclamation and discussed Fair Housing month. 
Supervisor Thompson made a motion  to approve a Proclamation declaring April 2016 Fair Housing 
Month for Navajo County; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number 06-16, affirming Navajo County’s 
commitment to the principle of Fair Housing: Olivia Todd discussed the item.  Supervisor Whitesinger 
made a motion to adopt Resolution Number 06 -16, affirming Navajo County ’s commitment to the 
principle of Fair Housing; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the 
motion. Supervisor Thompson spoke about those individuals who have lost their homes.  
c. Consideration and possible approval of the sale of a vehicle to the Rim Country Senior Center for the sum 
of $1.00: Olivia Todd discussed the item. Supervisor Williams recognized those volunteers from Rim

<!-- page 4 -->
Country Senior Center. He spoke about the Rim Country Senior Center being one of the jewels of that 
community. Supervisor Williams made a motion  to approve the sale of a vehicle to the Rim Country 
Senior Center for the sum o f $1.00; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson . Vote unanimous 
approving the motion. Chairman Whiting thanked those volunteers present today for all that they do.  
EMERGENCY MANAGEMENT:  
a. Consideration and possible award of Contract #F16 -01-04 to Aecom for Hazard Mitigation Plan Update 
in the amount of $30,000 plus applicable taxes: Mary Springer discussed the item. Supervisor Thompson 
made a  motion to award Contract F16 -01-04 to AECOM for Hazard Mitigation Plan Update in the 
amount of $30,000 plus applicable taxes ; motion seconded by Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous 
approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible award of Contract #F16-02-22 to BOLD Planning for Emergency Operations 
Plan (EOP) and Continuity of Operations Plan (COOP) Update in the amount of $44,800 for the EOP and 
$52,800 for the COOP plus applicable taxes: Mary Springer discussed the item. Supervisor Whitesinger 
made a motion to award Contract #F16-02-22 to BOLD Planning for Emergency Operations Plan (EOP) 
and Continuity of Operations Plan (COOP) Update in the amount of $44,800 for the EOP and $52,800 for 
the COOP plus applicable taxes; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the 
motion.  
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE:  Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Whitesinger spoke about being a guest speaker at a pep ra lly for AzMerit at JFK in Cedar Creek. 
She thanked Danny Shurter and his team for their help with hosting the White Mountain Regional Transportation 
Partnership Meeting regarding soliciting dollars for the communities and the region. She spoke of a meeting with 
the Bureau of Indian Affairs . She thanked Catrina  Jenkins and Mary Springer for the community outreach in 
Cibecue for Ready Navajo County. She spoke about Naked Mobile Keeping Kids Safe Tour and was impressed 
with the amount of people who came to participate in that conversation.  She spoke abo ut how Nak ed Mobile 
Keeping Kids Safe Tour utilizes the Victim Advocacy Center in Show Low to help children throughout Navajo 
County and surrounding regions who have been abused . She thanked sta ff for hosting the budget meeting. 
Supervisor Black reported on the Tribal Outlook Economic Conference in Flagstaff. He also spoke about an 
invitation from Lena Fowler to attend a meeting regarding Navajo Generating Station. He further spoke about the 
Kayenta Regional Health Center opening on April 2 7 and invited all to attend. He invited staff to attend Shonto 
Rock Art Festival and Justice Day in Kayenta. Supervisor Williams reported on the Webelos Pack Den meeting 
in Clay Springs he attended at which he held a mock Board meeting  with the scouts . He further spoke about a 
meeting with Senator McCain and Senator Flake discussing the forest energy. Supervisor Thompson  spoke 
about a shooting in Winslow and the many conversations he has had with the families and emergency personnel. 
He spoke about the passing of Thomas Walker, Sr., father to Birdsprings’ Chapter President. He spoke about the 
Law and Justice Day on April 1 st that Navajo County co -hosted in Dilkon. He spoke about attending meetings 
regarding the concerns of the recent Presidential Preference Election that he will discuss with our Election 
Officials. He further spoke about how Dilkon would like to relocate the elections back to the Chapter House. He 
spoke about a meeting with NACOG.  James Jayne acknowledged Mary Springer, Catrina Jenkins, and their team 
for what they are doing with Emergency Management.  Brad Carlyon  spoke about Supervisor Williams’s son 
and his track season. Supervisor Williams spoke briefly about his children and their track activity. Supervisor 
Williams asked for prayers for Joyce  Hill. Chairman Whiting  spoke about a tour with the Emergency 
Management Team going to city councils and talking about forest restoration and fire preparedness. He spoke of 
the Real AZ meeting this week on Thursday regarding the modern day forest restoration. He invited everyone to 
the Victim’s Rights Symposium. He reported on the Joseph City Founder’s Day parade and events. He further 
thanked everyone for their help with hosting Senator Flake and Senator McCain. He spoke about the tour of

<!-- page 5 -->
Apache Railway and that the senators asked that every employee come out, and the employees were individually 
thanked for all they did towards keeping Apache Railway open.  
 
ADJOURN at 10:55 a.m.  
APPROVED:       DATED:  April 12, 2016 
 
Jason E. Whiting, Chairman 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors 
 
ATTEST: 
 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors
